279. **THE THREE RULES, AND THE PARAGRAPH UNDER THEM, AND THE UNICLAUSE.** Entered in the day-book of the Bellweather Claims Archive on the sixteenth of Longlight, YR 313, sixth hour, **against the office and against no person, in a fair copy, with the hour, signed by the keeper, which is not a thing a keeper does.** *One.* A cost is entered before it is incurred, in the book of the person who pays it and not the book of the person who decides it, and an entry made before is not a promise and is not a confession. *Two.* A thing that nobody dissents about is entered as though somebody had dissented, **with the name and the hour** — the name of the thing and never the name of a person, because a keeper who enters a dissent on behalf of a person is making a record and not a record of a person. *Three.* An authority is entered with the words that made it, and an authority that nobody can name the source of is entered as a habit, **and a habit is a thing a county does to itself when nobody is looking.** **The last clause is Dain's, said in a yard on Weir Street on the first of Embermonth, YR 312, in front of four men, at about the seventh hour, and Mara Vey has it in her own hand in four lines copied six months ago and has never told him so, and neither of them credits it to anybody and neither of them is going to, and a later writer may not have either of them say it out loud in this volume.** **Beneath the rules, in the same ink, is the required statement of what the rules are for: that they were made because of the ninth entering of the not asking on that page, that they do not end it, that a rule about what is written down is not a rule about what is done, that a procedure built around a thing that is not being said is a procedure and not a cure, and that the day this office stops entering the not asking is not the day the rules have done their work and this office will not know which of the two days it has reached. The concealment becomes a habit on the page, in a document, and not in a scene, and this is the volume's quietest page.**

280. **THE THIRD REASON NOBODY TELLS ANYBODY, AND THE SENTENCE THE BATCH ENDS ON.** Out of a fair copy of four lines in a minute of a bench in a county four hundred miles the other way: *we did not lose our register, we lost our refusal, a register is what you have instead.* **A county that was cut off has no office, so a line written in it cannot be served, cannot be answered and cannot travel, and that is why a promise kept a hundred and forty times in a field is the only durable record in that countryside: it is the only one that does not need an office to be true, because the men who swore it are still alive and can be asked.** The reason nobody in this county knows that is the same sentence, and the reason it has been working for nineteen years and four and a half years and has never been found out is the fourth half: **AN INSTRUMENT THAT DOES NOT WORK IS NEVER HEARD FROM.** **A later writer may not give him the reason the County of Ash's road does not answer. That is the fourth line of the notice in Chapter 140 and it is not his to know in Block 1; the notebook says so in Chapter 110 and the sentence is: he has had the answer since the first of Embermonth of the year before and it has never once told him why the second route does not answer, and he has stopped asking, and that is the part of the habit a rule about authority cannot reach.**

281. **THE YARD'S CONTRACT, COSTED, AND WHAT THE YARD LOST.** **Ninety working days; four men at the yard's rate of eight pence and a boy at four, being half; thirty-six pence a day; two thousand eight hundred and eighty and three hundred and sixty is three thousand two hundred and forty pence, footed on the page before the total is said aloud, and three shillings a day is a good day's work in a market town.** The Marches' own valuation: **two hundred and eighty-eight bushels of salt at nine pence is two thousand five hundred and ninety-two, and two hundred and sixteen bushels of seed at three pence is six hundred and forty-eight, and the two together are exactly the contract sum.** A bushel of salt at the Old Toll is three shillings, which is thirty-six pence, which is four times their nine; a bushel of seed has **no price in this county and the yard refuses to write one.** The yard takes the salt, is outargued on the seed, and takes it anyway, in front of four men, on its own grounds and not out of charity. **The one fifth — six hundred and forty-eight pence — is payable on the return, on a day the Marches name, and the guild's first hand gave that clause up in a gateway for a reason about a promise, and asked that it be noticed in a room, and it is not in any book.** The sheet was signed by the yard on the twenty-seventh and goes out on the Saturday; **it is a contract and not an acceptance, because the other end has not said yes, and a contract accepted by one end of a line is not a contract.** The yard's paper is in a minute book in a chamber over a tithe barn four hundred miles off and the yard's book holds the fact of it and not the paper. **Pei Tarrow is one of the four men and the boy of fifteen is not going, and that was said out loud in a yard in Chapter 110 and entered with no figure and no line made for it.**

282. **TOVAN'S FIFTH LINE, AND WHAT THE TWO BROTHERS ARE CONCEALING FROM EACH OTHER.** **The week-book for the twenty-third of Longlight has four lines with figures and a fifth with none, and the fifth is about the crossing out of the ninth of Embermonth, the eighty-eight days, and a date in his own account that is not this county's date, and it says: *he does not know I have entered it, and I am not going to tell him, and nobody in this county is ever going to ask to see this page.*** **Tovan has not told his brother and does not know that his brother has an envelope with a rank in it, and Aren has not told his brother about the offer and does not know that his brother has a page of his own. The two concealments are separate and both stand, and Chapter 110's list is written from Aren's side and states only what Aren holds.** **The estrangement is not healed. Toban's sentence is not re-said, improved on, or alluded to as a thing to be taken back, and the fourth of Hearthmonth's gate sentence and the twenty-fourth of Greenmonth's yard sentence are both spent and neither is in these ten chapters.**

283. **THE BREACH AT THE END OF CHAPTER 110: OPEN, AND UNCHANGED IN SHAPE.** **He gives her the numbers and not the morning. She has known there is a morning since the fifth of Greenmonth, YR 313 and has not asked about it nine times, and the ninth is entered on the sixteenth with no reason on the line and none going on there.** He has not told her about the offer, and she has not asked, and neither of them has said a thing that could be called an apology: she names the refusal in advance, *if you say thank you I have to write down that you said thank you, and if you say you are sorry I have to write down that you said you are sorry.* The five documents about each other are now **six**: a notebook in a coat; a day-book over a weigh-house; a letter in a guild hall with two holders; four lines copied in her own hand six months ago; a sheet with twelve months on it that she wrote out and tore up in front of him; **and a fair copy of three rules, signed by her, which anybody in the county may come up two flights and read.** **Volume 04's chosen intimacy is bought with this and no scene in Volume 03 may buy it early.**

284. **THE BELL, AND WHY IT GOES ON A THURSDAY.** **The arrangement is a Monday, and it has been a boy and a ferrywright since the twenty-fourth of Fallowmonth and a boy and a carpenter since the ninth of Hearthmonth, and it is a Monday because that is the day the far end of the line keeps, and it is in a page at the head of the yard's book with the hour against it in a hand somebody would know, and nobody wrote a word in it about who rang the bell and nobody has written a word in it about who arranged it.** **On the thirtieth of Longlight, a Thursday, the boy of sixteen rang it anyway, and said the wharf was loud and he wanted to see whether the far end heard it on the wrong day, and the carpenter said that was not a reason and held the line.** **At the far end nothing was struck, because it is a Monday arrangement, and two people stood on a coping and waited and went in, and neither of them has ever been asked a question about it by anybody.** **The arrangement is not Aren's. He does not ring it, he does not go up the road, and nothing in the sound is his; and for the first time in three months nothing at the far end of it is anybody's either. The tile on the coping is not touched, not looked at, not named, and its device is named by nobody, and no Volume 03 batch may schedule an explanation, a flashback, an origin, a maker or a deathbed for it.**

285. **THE INSTRUMENT OF THE NINTH OF LONGLIGHT, NOW ON THE PAGE, AND ITS UNKNOWN OBJECT.** **A Crown instrument of the ninth of Longlight, YR 312, in an office's hand, in force, not superseded, barring the assembly of any body of this county in respect of the object named in it. It is one of the four lawful or unanswerable Crown instruments in this county, it was described back to a room in Merefen on the twelfth of Greenmonth, and nobody in this county can say what its object is.** **It is printed at the foot of Dain's standing offer in Chapter 105 and he reads it four times and cannot find the object, and writes four candidates in a notebook and crosses out *ask somebody who is not me*, on the ground that there is nobody in this county who is not him at anything. This is a live question the block opens and does not answer, and it is the volume's political pressure in its plainest form: a delegation sat in a hall over a wharf on the ninth of this month in a room of forty people, and whether that was an assembly of a body of this county is a question nobody has asked, and the delegation has gone home.**

286. **THE COURT'S TWO INSTRUMENTS, AND WHY A MAGISTRATE IS AFRAID.** **On the nineteenth of Longlight, on a Sunday, Lysa Fenn notes a boundary of thirty-one years for a shilling where the plan fee is four shillings and eightpence, entering the shilling against the court and not against the man, and the reeve's office calls a one-shilling note a discouragement and is right about the arithmetic and wrong about what a court is for.** On the same bench, in the same week, in the same hand, she spends the Marches' paper in four minutes with the construction she used on the ninth of Greenmonth: no person is in default, nothing is pending, the instrument is spent. **The two facts are one sentence: a person can be heard, refused and appealed, and a thing that cannot be refused and cannot be appealed is not a court and is a form.** And the fear, dated and unanswerable: *a body is not a person* is the sentence she has won eleven arguments with, and on the ninth of Greenmonth this county made the Bellweather Claims Archive a body corporate with a door, a number and the capacity to sue and to be sued, **and she cannot keep the first half of her own reasoning and drop the second half, and she has looked for the sentence that lets her for an hour and it is not there, and that is written on the back of a paper on a Sunday where nobody will find it unless somebody goes looking, which is a thing she has now done once.** **A body corporate is a thing that can be got at, and she made it, and the volume's next-volume question — that an oath renewed in public by men who are still alive is the only durable consent a rural county can produce and also the only document in the countryside that names a body, and a body can be served — is the shape of this and may not be stated in these ten chapters or in the next ten.**

287. **THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS, IN ONE PLACE, AND WHAT A LATER WRITER MUST NOT UNDO.** **A review of the ten chapters returned nine blockers, nineteen major findings and eighteen nits. Every blocker and every major is applied. No plot beat was removed, no chapter renumbered, no card broken, no date the batch is built on moved, and no controller file touched.** The batch is **31,648 words** after the pass against **31,680** before it, and the per-chapter column is **3217 / 2946 / 3004 / 3397 / 3475 / 3237 / 2868 / 3038 / 3082 / 3370**, which foots. **The nine blockers.** (1) and (2) Two narrator-voice references to the book outside a physical object in Chapter 104 — a chapter number in a man's speech and the phrase *not in this chapter* — and both are cut. (3) **The Ash figure nine hundred and forty-one spoken in a room over a weigh-house in Chapter 103, and called people, and set beside a Merefen bench: deleted.** (4) ***None* written in a box headed *State the office, if any* in Chapter 105, which Chapter 51 forbids and which destroyed the chapter's own motif: the box is empty on the page and Aren's prose says it has always been empty.** (5) **The return's columns described three different ways across Chapters 102 and 105, including a sentence that contradicted itself inside itself: all three now agree with Chapter 51 and with each other**, and the chapter says in Chapter 105 that the entry beside the fourth column is Rill's own hand on the back of the leaf and the third column is the printed example. (6) **Chapter 108 said the total before the column was footed, and called thirty-six pence a day *a mark and a half* when a mark is forty-eight pence: the footing now comes first and the gloss is three shillings.** (7) **The concealment between the brothers was reversed between Chapters 108 and 110: Chapter 110 now holds Chapter 108's state and the two concealments are separate.** (8) **The leaf arrived on three different days across three chapters: it arrives on the fourth and every age reference has been re-derived, and Salter's letter is dated the twenty-ninth of Greenmonth.** (9) **The delegation left on the wrong day: the eighteenth, not the sixteenth, in three places.** **The majors applied.** The Marches' oath made four hundred years old instead of a hundred and forty; the guild eighteen men in one line and nineteen in two; the Old Toll dressed block eleven weeks old when it is sixteen weeks since the fourth of Hearthmonth, and a wet place watched for a fortnight corrected to sixteen weeks; **Wend's column four thousand and thirty-two lines when twice a day for eleven years is eight thousand and thirty-one**; **a bench with four marks and six a year and not eleven marks**; **the breach's two anchor dates, which are the fifth of Greenmonth and not the twenty-fourth of Greenmonth and not the twentieth of Fallowmonth**; the served schedule about eight months old and not nine; the delegation eighteen days and not nine in two places; **the Archive four hundred yards from the watch house and not four hundred miles**; **Chapter 110's bell moved off Monday, and the boy is now shown ringing it on the wrong day on purpose with nothing struck at the far end, which is the only repair that keeps the far end's Monday**; **Chapter 110's notebook no longer states why the County of Ash's road does not answer, because that is Chapter 140's fourth line**; and **the drift warning restored to its own line in the notebook — *A road held, three weeks* — with the road formula left unsourced, which is where it has always been.** **Judged and recorded rather than changed.** Forty-four instances of *four hundred miles*, cut from fifty where a noun would do and left where the distance is the volume's load-bearing fact or where a Marches voice is speaking. Fifty-three bolded narrator section headers in ten chapters, which is fewer per chapter than Volume 02's last block and is the manuscript's section device, so the count is not treated as a defect. Thirteen *about n seconds or minutes* constructions, all of them load-bearing, against Volume 02's ten.

## Relationship state at the end of Chapter 110

- **Aren and Mara Vey: the breach, unrepaired, and it is now a procedure.** Eleven days into the volume and the two of them have done a piece of work together that is not about a wound, and it is the three rules in the day-book, and it is a **procedure built around the concealment and not an end to it, and the page under the rules says so in her own hand.** He gives her the numbers and not the morning. She has known since the fifth of Greenmonth that there is a morning and has not asked nine times, and the ninth is entered with no reason on the line. She names the refusal in advance so that neither of them can say thank you or sorry. **He has a dated offer from a Crown office in a sealed envelope two hundred miles off and she does not know, and she has entered that she did not ask, and neither of them has said a word about the offer.** **The documents about each other are six and the sixth is the fair copy of the three rules, which anybody in this county may come up two flights and read, and which contains a sentence about a habit that both of them know is about him and neither of them credits to anybody.** The tile on the coping is not touched and is not mentioned to her. **No apology, no question about the morning, no misunderstanding made available, no romantic consolation, in any of the ten chapters. Volume 04's chosen intimacy is bought with this and nothing in Volume 03 may buy it early.**
- **Aren and Tovan Kest: two unreported things and a contract.** The estrangement is not healed and is not touched. **Tovan has a fifth line in his own week-book with an hour against it and no figure, about a date in the wrong calendar, and has not told his brother and is not going to.** **Aren has an offer, and a leaf in a press, and a row of files with a figure against it, and has told nobody, and the list of the six he told nothing to is in his notebook.** Tovan's yard has signed for four hundred miles of river work and is four days from sending four men and a bag of salt, and the boy of fifteen is not going, and **neither brother is told anything by the other in these ten chapters, and the reason nobody in Merefen has told anybody anything is the reason Volume 02 found and it is now written down in a notebook in caps: an instrument that does not work is never heard from.**
- **Aren and Lysa Fenn: not met in this block, and each has independently concluded the same thing about an instrument.** She has spent a Sunday discovering that *a body is not a person* and *this county has a person* cannot both stand, and she does not know he is the man who read a form's foot this month and wrote four candidates in a notebook and crossed out the words *ask somebody who is not me*. He has not been in the moot hall. The four pence he is owed and the six pence the county paid a man twice are both on the page and neither of them knows about the other.
- **Aren and Reeve Aldis Fenn: the fourth asking, and a box with a name in a minute.** He is in the room on the thirtieth of Greenmonth and again in the notebook on the thirtieth of Longlight and not in either scene of hers. She has asked the minute to carry three things, the third of which is a man paid six pence twice out of a surveyor's own box, and the minute carries it, and she has voted for a thing four times knowing it is a subscription, and neither of them says anything to the other about any of it.
- **Aren and Sabin Dain: an offer, a note, and no conversation.** **Three enclosures, one of which is her own and is not the office's, and she has told him in writing that she does not believe it will work and that she has not said so to her own office.** He has read the marginal instruction three times in his life and understood the office in it this morning out of a line of office furniture at the foot of a sheet, *Retained. Series R.C. Eastern. Two classes*, and has written it down nowhere. **He has not written back. He has not told anyone about any of it. She is the only living person in this case who has told him, in writing, not to take something, and he has not yet decided, and the refusal is four chapters from the end of the volume.**
- **Aren and the hand of the Ninebridge: named three times, on a road he does not hold, in a notebook and in a bell.** She is the woman who holds the other end of a line, the tile-cutter of forty-one is a man in the same sentence, and on the thirtieth of Longlight **nothing was struck at the far end at all**, because a boy of sixteen rang a bell on the wrong day to find out. She has not been asked a question about the road and did not ask one. The tile on the coping is not touched and not named and its device is named by nobody.
- **Mara Vey: the six names, the seven instruments, and the four words.** She has entered a name in her book for the first time in four months, Oswin Rill's, against a person, with a claim number against nothing, and a second time the minute-writer of a council in a border county, with a date and a place. She has a whole leaf of a return in her press, un-copied, and four words on its wrapper, and a fair copy of six lines off a board that cannot be copied out of, and a signed sheet of three rules anybody may read. **She has three instruments and no fourth: a press that cannot be served, a letter that a person could answer, and the day-book. She did not write the letter, and the reason is in the entry, and the reason is that she will not trade the only protection this office has for a sentence she would like to have said.** She has not asked about the morning for the ninth time and entered it, and she has not asked about the offer because she does not know of it, and the day this office stops entering the not asking is not the day anything has changed.
- **Mara Vey and the man at a kiln: not mentioned in these ten chapters, and not asked about, and the eighth month now behind it.** He is four hundred miles off in a county of clay and he is still a number, and the woman who reads for a living has a whole leaf of a different kind in her press, with a man's own hand on the back of it, and she is not going to be the person who notices one.
- **The Marches council and the Marches bridge guild: met, reasonable, better than the north, and gone home on the eighteenth.** Neither is a rescue and neither is a villain, and one of the four voices in the room is a woman of thirty-eight who has never been asked anything and a man of sixty who gave away six hundred and forty-eight pence in a gateway for a reason about a promise, and a seed-carter who says the price of what he wants out loud in a room because a price is worth nothing in a village. **The guild's first hand and the woman who renews the stone are both still unnamed on the page and are Batch 0012's to name.**

## Canon established by Batch 0012 (Volume 03, Chapters 111–120, prose on the page)

**How to use this section.** Items 1–254 are Volume 01, the Volume 02 plan, and Chapters 51–100. Items 255–269 are the Volume 02 close. Items 270–287 are Batch 0011. **Items 288 onward are Batch 0012, the second block of Volume 03, and they are the canon Chapters 121–150 are written against. Nothing in this section re-derives an earlier item; where it disagrees it says so in the item.**

288. **The block, in one place.** Ten chapters, **29,130 words**, main world to the twenty-second of Rainmonth and then three path-time chapters, **one crossing out, no return, one System-facing line of any kind in the whole block: none.** POVs Aren ×6, Mara Vey ×1, Lysa Fenn ×1, Reeve Aldis Fenn ×1, Tovan Kest ×1, Pei Tarrow ×1. **Two new names and four slots unfilled, as the cast plan allows: Coll Ferrow and Delya Munn. The volume total is four of seven and the unfilled slots are sixteen of twenty-three.** Nobody dies, nobody is killed, no arch goes down, no glass breaks, the tile on the coping is not touched or looked at or named, the eighth ruled line is not looked at, the brine weights are not mentioned or filed, no Ash figure is on the page in any form, and *Culling Order*, *custodian*, *Bind* and *Gatehouse* do not appear.

289. **THE SIX TERMS ARE NOW ON THE PAGE THREE TIMES, IN THEIR FIXED WORDING, AND A LATER WRITER MAY NOT RE-WORD ONE.** Printed in full in Chapter 104 off the board, **again in full in Chapter 114 off the keeper's fair copy, in the same order, character for character**, and quoted in part in Chapters 112, 115, 118 and 120. **Term four is the price and term six is the door, and the first review pass found Chapter 118 with them inverted and it is corrected.** Term two is *no name is to be read out on the far side of the line by anybody from the north, and no name is to be written in the line's book at the north end* — **and that is why the line's book at the north end has four columns, a fifth column under a ruled line for the hour, and no column for a name, which is now on the page three times.**

290. **THE NINE, AND THE SEVEN, AND WHY.** *A line. A hand at this end. A hand at that end. A date. The hour. A witness at this end who is not the man who keeps the road. A witness at that end who is not the man who keeps that road. The book at this end. The book at that end.* Printed in full in Chapter 112 and referred to in Chapter 115 and worked entry by entry in Chapter 118. **Seven of the nine can be made at the north end and the two that cannot are the date and the book at the far end.** A later writer may not say eight. The two hands are **Coll Ferrow at the Marches' end and the man at the drawknife at the north end** — **the man at the drawknife is deliberately unnamed, is one of Toban's four, went in on the fifteenth on the Marches' road, and is the yard's arithmetic hand; Tovan Kest does not go and must not be named as a hand at an end of a line he is not at.**

291. **THE RETURNED SHEET, AND THE DAY IT NAMES, AND WHY NOTHING NAMED A DAY FOR A LINE.** A sheet from the council and the bridge guild of the Hearth Marches, signed at the foot **Coll Ferrow**, in a good hand, **sixteen days on the road and dated in the Marches' own frame as the nineteenth day of the rolling that ends with the sowing.** It says: the line is not a road; **a day is named for four men of a yard and the day is the day those four men will be on that county's road, and no day has been named for the line and there will not be one until the line is made**; a stranger's labour is paid in the county's coin at the crossing and not in goods; and the woman of thirty-eight of Sallow Marsh has not given a line of her book. **A message is addressed to a person and a copy is addressed to a shelf**, and a sheet that names one of the four things a road is, is a message and not a route.

292. **THE ROAD DOES NOT OPEN, AND NOBODY SAYS THE WORD.** **Crossing A went out on the twenty-second of Rainmonth at the fourth hour, on a day the Marches had not named, and the Marches named a day for four men of a yard and not for the line, so term six stops it, and the second route did not answer at all inside the ten days.** He cannot tell whether it was refused or merely not kept, there is no procedure for a far side that has not decided, and **he wrote the rule on the page in his own hand: nobody in either country is to say the word about it, not afterwards, not kindly, not in a letter, not in a book, not in a minute, not in nine years — a road that does not open on a day is a day that was not opened, and the moment a person puts a word on it the word is a thing somebody chose, and a thing somebody chose is a thing somebody can be shown.** **This is the first crossing in this case that has come back with no answer of any kind, and the whole of the next nine months happens without one.** No character in Chapters 111–120 uses the word *failed* about a road.

293. **THE GAUGE COLUMN, IN FULL, AND THE GAP IN IT.** **Nine gauges on a river of that size, nine posts, nine keepers, nine books, and one book in a hut at a weir that is not a county book.** Thessa Wend has read the weir post and the Sallow foot **twice a day since the first sowing, eleven years, and the book has eight thousand and thirty-one lines in it**, and the last line is the day she started, which she did not know was the first one. Hand: small, quick, slightly left-sloping, worse in the wet; the first four hundred lines neat and the last thousand not; a whole season written smaller a third of the way in because she had been ill. **In the middle of it, nine lines in a different hand and a different kind of entry: a week she was not in the county for, a ditch said to have been cut and not seen cut, a field under water to the third row, and *whoever reads this, there is a week in this book that is not in the book, and I have left the space for eleven years.*** **Nobody has ever asked her for a page. The council has never looked at it. It is a claim and not a record because there is no person in it to be contradicted.** Her reason for the gap is on the page and is the volume's: **a gauge read twice a day by the man whose land it is under is not a gauge, it is a thing he has agreed with.**

294. **THE WHITE GROUND, THE STRUCK, AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY.** Four villages, ground the colour of a bone, wet underfoot, the grit not sand. **About sixty households on the council's roll and not on the oath, not because anybody struck them, because the oath is renewed in a field in public by the men who hold the water, and the woman on three hearths does not hold water, and there is no form in that county that says a woman may hold a ditch.** She has lived on the ground forty-one years and has never been asked what she swore, because she never swore anything, and she would tell him if he asked, and nobody has. **And she says, unasked, what his county will do with her: *you will write it down and you will say a woman of about sixty was on three hearths and was not on the oath, and that is all that will be said, and it will be said in a room where there is nobody from here, and in nine years a man will read it and think he knows something.*** **The book must not say the struck of the Marches are a mirror of the struck in Ash and no figure from one is ever set beside a figure from the other.**

295. **THE MECHANISM, SEEN AND NOT SAID, AND THE AUNT.** **A bridgewright saw it in a day and a half: the ditch eleven yards off the trough, and the downstream bank gone over on the same line, by the same men, in the same month, at the same stage of the water, for a hundred and forty years — a hand's width a year.** He says nothing, in that county, to anybody, and writes *seen, not told, and not to be said in this county by me*, **because of the third of the six — the north is not to send anybody who can be asked to come and look — and because a man who has been in a county five days with a bag is exactly the man that line is about, and because the woman with eleven years of numbers has never once been asked for them, and if the mechanism leaves this county in a gateway it will be his and it will be in a book and it will be a thing a keeper could not contradict.** **The mechanism is said in trade terms, in Chapter 121 onward, by the woman with the gauge column and not by a stranger, and it is not said in these ten chapters by anybody.**

296. **THE REFUSAL, AND THE WORD FOR IT.** He offered four terms; the fourth was that the water be read and a true copy of a month of the readings sit in the line's book at the north end, where anybody in this county could come up two flights and read. **It breaks the second of the six, because a column with a woman's name at the head of it is a name.** The road was refused **on the terms he offered and accepted on the terms it offered**, and there was no vote, and the refusal was one question, asked by **Thessa Wend, who has never been asked anything, in about four seconds: *how many people in your county are going to read it.*** He had to answer that he did not know, and that there is a woman in a room over a weigh-house who does not know this county exists and whom he has not told. **And a county that has never refused anything has no word for refusing; the word it has is the word for a thing that got lost — and a thing that got lost can be looked for and a thing that has been refused cannot, and the whole of their law is that you may ask for a thing back.** The minute enters it as a fact and not as a defeat. **The refusal is correct, and the Marches are right, and no one in this volume may call any of them a villain.**

297. **DELYA MUNN, AND THE STONE, AND WHAT SHE IS RIGHT AND WRONG ABOUT.** Fifty-five, of that council, renews the oath-stone in public, **and is the person who will not have it lifted**, and has been asked twice and has said no twice and put the reason in the minute: **a thing a hundred and forty years old that nobody can lift is the only piece of evidence this county has ever had that anybody agreed to anything, and if it goes into a cart it goes into a cart with a hole in it.** She is **right about the history and wrong about the soil**, she is the one who says in Block 3 that there is a working that would take the water out of a ditch in an afternoon, and **she does not say it in these ten chapters.** The stone was sworn on the eighth day **in a field he was not in**, which was the first time the fourth of the six had been kept in four weeks, and it was kept because a stranger was in the county, and she came and told him the hour and did not ask him to come.

298. **THE SIX HUNDRED AND FORTY-EIGHT, AND WHY IT IS THE SAME NUMBER TWICE AND NOT THE SAME MONEY.** Footed in Chapter 116, in the yard's book, in pence, before the total is said: **2,880 + 360 = 3,240** (the yard's own figure for four men and a boy at ninety days); the county pays **288 bushels of salt at 9 = 2,592**; **2,880 − 2,592 = 288**, what the four men are short by at the county's price for its own salt; **288 + 360 = 648**; **and 648 is the last fifth of the whole sum, which the guild's first hand retained on the twenty-third of Longlight and then gave up in a gateway in Weir Street for a reason about a promise.** **So a man of sixty gave away, in a gateway, exactly what the yard is out of pocket by, and he is four hundred miles off, and the yard's book holds the fact of that and not the paper.** 288 pence is **six marks, or six shillings a man**, and the crew's own road keep for eleven days is **352 pence, seven marks and sixteen pence**, out of the yard, because the sheet pays the crossing and not the road. **The Chapter 10 column of eleven marks and fourpence and the Chapter 108 figures are untouched and are not re-derived and nothing is scaled out of either.**

299. **THE SECOND COUNT, AND THE NUMBER NOBODY ASKED FOR.** A proposed count of persons oath-bound in a border county, by the county's own schedule and by the stones where the county keeps its stones, **dated the twenty-eighth of Rainmonth's predecessor, returnable on or before the twenty-second, with a ruled space for a figure and no column anywhere on the sheet that a name could be put in.** In the same bag, dated the twenty-sixth, **the day before it was asked for**, in a woman's small upright hand: **nine thousand two hundred and sixty for a district of eleven counties**, with three lines under it, and the second of the three is the volume's political fact — **one of the eleven has returned nothing in eleven years and is returned here at the average of its neighbours.** A county that has never sent a return has been returned, at an average, so that the total is smooth and no line shows a place where a place ought to be; **nobody has done anything wrong and there is no officer to blame**, and the office cannot say whose figure the average is, in the same way and for the same reason it cannot say whose figure a printed warning is. The third line: *this is not an instrument, it requires no answer, it names no person, it cannot be refused, it is a figure and it will be used.* **The count was answered on the morning of the twenty-second with five hundred and ninety-seven — the persons entered in the register of the Bellweather Claims Archive since the register was begun, footed in eleven columns twice in two orders and both run-totals on the sheet, of whom not one is in a border county and not one is oath-bound — and the instrument was spent the moment it was answered, and the construction took four minutes having taken nine the first time, and she wrote the four down because a thing that gets faster is a thing being learned and not argued.** **No name in a Crown's hand in this county for the second time. The 1,609 of Chapter 93 is a different figure, is not to be disputed and is not to be broken, and appears nowhere in these ten chapters.**

300. **THE REPLY TO SABIN DAIN, WHICH NEITHER ACCEPTS NOR REFUSES, AND NOBODY KNOWS.** A quarter-sheet into the bag for the provincial town, two hundred miles off, on the thirteenth of Rainmonth, **in this county's hand, with no returnable date, no claim number and no copy in this county, and none of those three facts is an accident.** It says a warden's rank is a command and not a custody; that a sealed record of a person is a record a person cannot contradict; **that he is not able to say he will take the offer and not able to say he will not, because both would be a promise made by a man who does not know what the eastern paths are**; and, under it, *a word on what the office is buying* — the office is not recruiting what he can do, it is recruiting what a form did to him, and it worked that out from its own paperwork. **It does not use the word *custodian*. It does not explain the marginal instruction. Dain does not change her want and is not the person who explains anything, and she is not in these ten chapters at all.** Dain's offer is refused in writing in Chapters 148–150 and not in this block.

301. **THE LINE'S BOOK, AND WHERE IT WAS RULED, AND WHAT IT COST.** Eleven inches by eight, **ruled on the eleventh of Longlight, in a room over a cooper, on the back of a cost sheet because there was nothing else of a size**, four columns with no headings, a fifth under a ruled line for the hour, and **no column for a name**. The three days of paid work in Chapter 115 were **not** the ruling: they were forty yards of tarred hemp off a cooper's bin laid along the Old Toll parapet, four marks, and a day of the man at the drawknife putting the two ends into the abutment. **Footed: four hands at eight pence for three days is 96, the boy at four pence for three days is 12, and 96 + 12 = 108 pence, which is nine shillings, which is two marks and twelve pence**, paid out of a chest that had two marks in it on the thirtieth of Greenmonth and is now lighter by the whole of it, and the reeve asked that the minute carry that too. **The hemp cost a pot of beer and the beer is in the minute, which is the first beer in that roll.** The cost of sending the column of eleven counties four hundred miles is **102 pence, the first thing that office has ever spent on a letter**, and the entry is against the office and against no person. **The line's book is the second of the case and the first ruled on a cost sheet; the room with a door on a lane is the Bellweather Claims Archive and no book in this case is ruled in it by him.**

302. **THE WITNESS AT THE NORTH END, AND WHY HE ASKED FOR IT IN ANOTHER HAND.** **Bram Ottery**, watch sergeant, no office, eleven years in that house, the witness at this end on the ninth of Embermonth of the year before, paid fourpence a week out of a private box, did not draw it, will not have it in the yard's book. He agrees on two conditions: he will be a man off a bridge and not a man in a guild, **because Aren is on the roll of the guild and does not keep any road, and a witness who is the man who keeps the road is a witness who can be asked what he thinks of him**; and he wants the fact in the yard's book in this county's hand and not his own, **because a clerk four hundred miles off copied his writing down once and not the daylight, and everything that came of it came of the copy.** The witness at the Marches' end was chosen by the Marches and **is not named on anything**.

303. **THE BREACH AT THE END OF CHAPTER 120: OPEN, AND THE COUNT IS STILL NINE.** The count of not-askings stays at **nine, entered on the sixteenth of Longlight**, and Chapter 111 has her say in advance that she is not entering a tenth about this, because entering a tenth would be entering a question. **A keeper who alters her own fair copy enters the alteration with the date and the hour, and so the marginal note she wrote against the sixth of the six — *a condition of this office, and the office does not know what it is a condition of* — is withdrawn in her own hand on the fourth of Rainmonth**, and the withdrawal is entered against the office and not against a person, and the entry beside it says that this office has been wrong in the same direction three times this year and has no procedure for it. **The third rule is a procedure built around a concealment and not an end to it and nothing in these ten chapters changes that.** He gives her a number and nothing else. **No apology in any register, no question about the morning, no misunderstanding made available, no romantic consolation, in any of the ten chapters. Volume 04's chosen intimacy is bought with this and no scene in this block buys it.**

304. **THE BELL, AND WHY IT GOES ON A MONDAY, AND WHAT CAME BACK.** On the fourth of Rainmonth, a Monday, the bell went on the Old Toll bridge at the fourth hour and a half, by the boy of sixteen and the carpenter, because that is the arrangement, and the arrangement is a Monday because that is the day the far end keeps. **The far end kept.** On a coping on a river this county does not have, two people — one a tile-cutter of forty-one, one the person who keeps that road — stood on it and did not strike it, and a sound came across in a second and a half that is not a bell and is not nothing, and **the same sentence went into the minute of that county's assizes in a clerk's flat hand, and it is the third time it has been entered**, including the clause that *the coping itself is not described in this entry because no person present has asked what is on it and none of them is charged with it*, which was in all three minutes and in none of the three clerks' hands. **A keeper of records in Merefen was told on the fifth and entered it with the hour against no person, and there is nothing on the coping she can be shown, because the coping is in another county and the sentence about it is in a minute in another county, and a minute is a fact about a room.** **The tile is not touched, not looked at, not named, its device is named by nobody, and no Volume 03 batch may schedule an explanation, a flashback, an origin, a maker or a deathbed for it.** The boy says he rang it on the wrong day on purpose and has not asked the carpenter about it, **because the carpenter will give him a straight answer and he would rather not have one until he knows what he thinks**, and he could have asked Aren and did not, because a boy of two years of an apprenticeship asking a man in a guild is a different question from a boy asking the page.

305. **THE DRIFT, WRITTEN ON THE FAR SIDE AND ON FAITH, AND THE ONLY FIGURE IN THE VOLUME A MAN CAN CHECK.** Chapter 120: **nine days on this side, counted off the sun twice and the shadow of a weir hut once; a road held, three weeks, printed at the foot of the terms in an office's hand, twenty-one days, unchanged in a year; ten days of absence against twenty-one is minus eleven days, and the warning is longer than the absence, and it is the first crossing in this case inside the printed warning.** **The error is the absence minus the printed warning and the path interval is nowhere in the sum** — the first pass of this batch subtracted the interval from the warning, which is the forbidden operation, and the correction is recorded here because a later writer must not repeat it. The entry is written on faith, because the ten is not known until the return, and the return is the second of Harvestmonth. **The ratio is the thing and not the figure: a quarter at twenty-six days, about three quarters at eighty-eight, more than one here, and it is not a constant; and a good result teaches a man nothing and a bad one teaches him everything, and this one he cannot do anything about because there is nothing wrong with it.** **The half he cannot check is the calendar, and the conversion is not attempted again in this block: a rolling here is four weeks and a month in the county he came out of is thirty days, and there is not one of those in the other, and the one conversion the volume attempts is Chapter 130's and is the plant.**

306. **THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS, IN ONE PLACE, AND WHAT A LATER WRITER MUST NOT UNDO.** A review of the ten chapters returned **nine blockers, twenty-two major findings and fifteen nits. Every blocker and every major is applied; no plot beat was removed, no chapter renumbered, no card broken, no date the batch is built on moved, and no controller file touched.** The batch is **29,130 words** after the pass. **The nine blockers, all applied.** (1) Two narrator-voice references to the book outside a physical object in Chapter 120 and one in Chapter 119 — *this chapter* three times — and this defect was found and removed once already in the block's predecessor. (2) **A main-world weekday written in a book, by a Marches character, in path time** — *Monday* inside the nine lines in Wend's hand — and it is now *in the week of the low water*. (3) **A path-time paragraph that converted the main-world calendar into a path date and was garbled into a tautology**, and the whole paragraph is cut; the one conversion the volume attempts is Chapter 130's. (4) **"Four months" spoken twice in path time**, including a sentence that contradicted the four-week renewal week. (5) **The drift figure was produced by the forbidden subtraction and did not foot** — nine against twenty-one is minus twelve, and the figure minus eleven is only reachable as ten against twenty-one. (6) **Terms four and six inverted in Chapter 118.** (7) **Chapter 118 named Tovan Kest as the hand at the north end** and said he had gone, contradicting Chapter 117, and he did not go. (8) **288 pence called five shillings**, and it is six marks, or six shillings a man. (9) **"Eight of the nine" stated three times over a page that shows two blanks**, and it is seven. **Among the majors applied:** sixteen days against seventeen on the road; a room over a cooper against a room with a door on a lane; five days in the county against a fortnight; the ninth of Longlight written as the fourth; eleven weeks for the age of the second of the six; the first drift figure claimed as a first in four years and a half when it is the first in this volume; **a sentence saying the ratio does not change in the same paragraph as a list of it changing**; **Chapter 120 re-running Chapter 110's three-times-failed conversion and pre-empting Chapter 130's plant**; a bell nine inches higher and not a foot lower; the county not knowing the yard's name against a sheet carrying it; the witness choosing Aren against Aren asking him; nine people in a hall against nine bench votes; nine of eleven in a room against eleven of eleven; a duplicated paragraph in Chapter 118 and a re-delivery of Chapter 104's wage paragraph in Chapter 120; **Chapters 112 and 115 staging the same scene twice, the ruling and the no-column reasoning and the nine-item list, now cut from 115 and referred back**; and the yard's contract sheet sitting on the bench-board in two chapters after Chapter 110 sent it out in a bag on the second of the month. **Judged and recorded rather than changed:** thirty-five bolded narrator headers and ten `---` rules, one rule per chapter, cut by two thirds from thirty-nine and thirty; twenty-three instances of *the whole of* against thirty-two; a boy of fourteen, a woman with a slate and a man of about fifty at a sluice, all addressed by description.

## Relationship state at the end of Chapter 120

- **Aren and Mara Vey: the breach, unrepaired, and now a habit that is on the page in a document twice.** He came up on the fourth of Rainmonth and gave her a number and nothing else — the twenty-second, at the fourth hour, a Friday, and not a market day, checked twice because he has lost a market twice by inattention — **and told her in the same breath that the sheet named a day for four men and did not name one for him, and she did not ask him the question because he had not made it a question.** **The count of not-askings is still nine, entered on the sixteenth of Longlight, and she says in advance that she is not entering a tenth about this, because entering a tenth would be entering a question, and because a rule about what is written down is not a rule about what is done.** On the same evening **she withdrew her own marginal note against the sixth of the six in her own hand, dated and entered, because it was wrong, and the withdrawal is the third document of theirs in a fair copy anybody may come up two flights and read.** The three rules are untouched and did their work and were not a cure. He has a reply in a bag for the provincial town that neither accepts nor refuses an offer she has no idea is in a coat, **and that reply is the fourth concealment and it is the first one that is in writing and on its way out of the county.** The tile on the coping is not mentioned to her. **No apology, no question about the morning, no misunderstanding made available, no romantic consolation, in any of the ten chapters, and he is now four hundred miles off inside a country that refused him.**
- **Aren and Tovan Kest: eleven words about a length of hemp, and a crew that went seven days earlier on somebody else's calendar.** The estrangement is not healed and is not touched. **Tovan has a fifth line in his own week-book with an hour against it and no figure, about a date of his brother's that is not this county's date, and he has now also written down the six hundred and forty-eight pence and what it is the same number as, and he has told the man at the drawknife and not his brother and has entered that he is not going to tell him.** **Aren has the offer, the leaf, the row of files and a reply to a Crown office in a bag, and has told nobody, and the list of six in his notebook has a seventh on it and one of the six is his brother.** **The yard is the only relationship in the book getting better and it got better by costing six hundred and forty-eight pence and refusing to let a boy of fifteen go, and by putting four men on a cart and going with them to a gate.** A peer of the wharf road says in a yard that the master's brother puts a bar through oak better than he does, and he agrees, and it goes on a tally-board in a yard's book, **and there are now two pages in two books in this town about the two of them and neither has ever been shown to the other.** Nobody in Merefen was told anything by anybody.
- **Aren and the hand of the Ninebridge: the far end kept, and nobody asked.** The bell went on a Monday and a Monday is the day the far end keeps, and something was struck on a coping on a river this county does not have and the same sentence went into a clerk's minute for the third time, including the clause that the coping is not described because nobody present has asked what is on it. **Mara Vey was told on the fifth, entered it with the hour against no person, and wrote the word *Monday* into her own book because a day of the week is the only part of it that is not a fact about two counties.** The tile is not touched and not named. **Aren was in a country on the other side of the case at the fourth hour of a day and did not call it, and what he did instead was write in a notebook that nobody in either country is to put a word on a road that did not open on a day.**
- **Aren and Lysa Fenn: a form, a foot-book and a brother at a sluice.** She spent the second instrument in four minutes and was frightened of the four, and a man of about fifty came to her door because a market had told him the Office was counting people, and she could not tell him and told him what she could, and he did not give a name and she did not ask for one and it went in a door-book with no column in it. **He was in the county on the ninth and did not come to the moot hall, and he went out of it on the twenty-second, and she does not know what he is carrying and there is no line in her book about him and there is not going to be one.**
- **Aren and Reeve Aldis Fenn: a fourth asking, a rubbing stopped, and a vote she is not on.** She has refused the witness line twice, word for word, in a gateway in Weir Street on the ninth of Embermonth of the year before and in a hall over a wharf on the twelfth of this month, and the only change is that she put her hand flat on the copy instead of putting the rule down, and she asked that it be minuted that she noticed the slot. The clerk enters *the reeve voted*. **The bench voted seven for and two against on the six lines on the twelfth, which is a first in the roll, and the reeve asked that the minute carry that the making of the line is three days of work and about two marks and twelve pence and there is no money in the room.** He was in the room and is on the roll and does not hold the crossing. Neither of them has said anything to the other about any of it.
- **Aren and Sabin Dain: a reply that neither accepts nor refuses, and nobody in this county knows it was written.** He told her what the office is buying and that it is not the thing he can do, and he did not use the word *custodian* and did not explain the clause, and he did not say thank you and did not say he was sorry. **She is not in these ten chapters and her want is unchanged and it has nothing to do with him, and he is four hundred miles off inside a county that refused him, and the refusal is four chapters from the end of the volume and is in a document.**
- **Aren and the Marches guild: refused on the terms he offered, accepted on the terms it offered, and no question asked of anybody afterwards.** **Coll Ferrow** named a day for four men of a yard and would not name one for a line, and stated the third of his terms again, and wants the fact that he called it the best thing anybody had put in the room minuted, because in four rollings somebody will tell his nineteen men that the stranger wanted a thing off them. **The guild did not work with him, was right not to, and is better than the north at the only thing that matters, which is water.** He is now in a country that has no word for refusing and has the word for a thing that got lost, and he has a mechanism he may not say and a book nobody has asked for and a question he could not ask.
- **Aren and Thessa Wend: the first person in the volume to be asked a question by a stranger, and the first one to refuse him.** He asked her for a line of the book and she gave him a number with a place on it. **On the sixth day she came down the bank and told him the book is hers and that a copy in a book four hundred miles off stops being a thing that can be contradicted and becomes a thing that is true, and a thing that is true does not need a person standing behind it.** Then she asked him how many people in his county are going to read it, in about four seconds, and it was the refusal, and she did not have to argue. **He wanted to ask her for a line of it and did not, and he cannot be told why without breaking the third of the six, and she has been waiting eleven years to be asked and was not, and she knows he wanted to, because she said so on the bank.** Nobody has ever asked her anything and she has now been asked and refused.
- **Aren and the woman of about sixty on three hearths: one exchange in a doorway, and she told him what he would do with her.** She is not on the oath and has never been asked what she swore. **She said: *you will go home and you will write it down and you will say a woman of about sixty was on three hearths and was not on the oath, and that is all that will be said, and it will be said in a room where there is nobody from here.*** He has not written it down and she said he would. This is the volume's next-volume question arriving in a doorway and it is not resolved in this block.
- **Aren and Delya Munn: one exchange, in a room, and she is right and he knows she is.** She refused the fourth term for a reason a hundred and forty years long, and **the reason she is right about is the history and the reason she is wrong about is the soil, and nobody in these ten chapters has said the second half out loud, including him, including in his notebook, which says *seen, not told*.** He will be in her room again in Chapter 124 and she will say the obvious thing there, and it will be right, and it will be wrong about the soil, and **no one in the volume may call her a villain.**
- **Mara Vey: the six names, the seven instruments, and a marginal note of her own struck out in her own hand.** She has entered a name in her book against a person for the first time in four months and a second time with a date and a place, and she has now **withdrawn an entry of her own, with the date and the hour, on the ground that it was wrong, and entered that this office has been wrong in the same direction three times this year and has no procedure for it.** She has a whole leaf of a return in her press, un-copied, with four words on the wrapper; a fair copy of six lines off a board that cannot be copied out of, with the six lines written out in full under them; a signed sheet of three rules anybody may read; a fair copy of a column of eleven counties with one question at the foot of it, sent to a shelf and not to a man; and a cost of one hundred and two pence entered against the office and against no person. **She has not asked about the morning for the ninth time and has entered the ninth, and she does not know there is an offer in a coat, and she does not know there is a reply in a bag, and the day this office stops entering the not asking is not the day anything has changed.**
- **Mara Vey and the man at a kiln: not mentioned in these ten chapters, and not asked about, and the ninth month is now behind it.** He is four hundred miles off in a county of clay and he is still a number, and the woman who reads for a living has a whole leaf of a different kind in her press with a man's own hand on the back of it, and she is not going to be the person who notices one.
- **The Marches council, the bridge guild, the bound and the ditchman, the boy of fourteen, and the woman of about sixty: all present, all reasonable, none of them a rescue and none of them a villain, and three of the four parties in this volume better than he is.** The council refused outside command a second time and meant it; the guild proved it knows the water better than the north and has said so since the first ten minutes and is right; **the man who keeps the minute writes facts and not decisions and entered a refusal as a fact and not a defeat;** the boy of fourteen reads a gauge and is taught to be looked at; and the woman of about sixty has three hearths and no form that says a woman may hold a ditch. **The salt is the villain of the volume and it is not a person and it is named as one by nobody.**
- **Tovan Kest and the yard: the only relationship in the book getting better, and it got better by costing money.** Four men and not a boy, ninety working days, salt and no seed, six hundred and forty-eight pence of exposure entered in the week-book with the hour, a pot of beer in a county minute, a boy of fifteen who stays and copies the empty spaces, and a man at the drawknife holding one end of a line four hundred miles off with no name in any book. **Toban's sentence in the yard is not re-said, improved on, or alluded to as a thing to be taken back, and no scene in this block heals the estrangement, and the yard is the only thing in the case that is better than it was a month ago.**

307. **THE SECOND REFUSAL, AND THE GUILD'S TERMS TAKEN INSTEAD.** Chapter 121: the council over the tithe barn refuses outside standing a second time, entered as fact with the hour, and accepts the guild's terms instead — water as somebody's job, stranger's labour at the guild's rate paid in county coin at the crossing, salt at nine pence the bushel. The rider is footed on a slate, 48 + 48 + 48 + 48 = 192 pence, and kept off the four white villages and off the sixty households on the roll and not on the oath, for the reason that the council has never asked those people for anything.
308. **THE MECHANISM SAID BY THE GAUGE WOMAN, AND NOT BY HIM.** Chapter 122: Thessa Wend says in trade terms that salt lay a foot and a half down since the ice and rises with water, and that the ditch stands eleven yards off the trough on a bank worn a hand's width a year for her eleven years walking it, and for as long as the stone has been kept. Chapter 120's seen, not told is honoured: he says nothing in the room and is not thanked for it. The volume question is asked in his notebook and not in the room: how to keep a promise kept a hundred and forty years that is ending its keepers.
309. **THE BROTHER AT THE WEIR.** Chapter 123: Perrin, twenty-two, drowned where the gauge-reader owned the water and signed the labour; Thessa tells it once, to him alone, as the reason she wants a reading by somebody who does not own the weir. She gives the council nine readings in her own hand and a leaf on the hand's width, dated by the county's own count. A later writer may not move the drowning, rename Perrin, or make the council hear it.
310. **THE WORKING NAMED ONCE AND REFUSED ONCE, WITH A DATE AND NO EXPLANATION.** Chapter 124: Delya Munn says there is a cut that would take the water out of that ditch in an afternoon, and the man off the road has done worse in a shorter time; Aren refuses, saying the reason is not a rule but that the last time one of these was cut the anchor tore and a community lost contact for ever, and the man was told afterwards the witness had been forged, and he wrote it down. Both lines stand once each and are not improved on. The word stands exactly twice as a noun in the block, both in this chapter, and nowhere else in Chapters 121–123 or 125–130. **Amended at the Batch 0013 review: the string `cut` occurs one further time in the block, at the end of Chapter 127, in `a line on the stone that nobody had cut`, which is the flood line on the chapel corner and not the working. A later phase grepping the word is to read that one before concluding anything, and the marking of stone in Chapter 127 is marked, lettered and chiselled and never cut.**
311. **THE SIXTY, COUNTED.** Chapter 125: the chest roll holds about sixty households of the four white villages with no oath mark, fifty-eight certain and two doubtful. The woman of sixty on three hearths hears the refusal through the boards. **Amended at the Batch 0013 review: the antithesis this item used to carry, that Ash removed from a count is a wound and the Marches unasked is a promise and neither is mended by answering, is no longer on the page in Chapter 125. The first pass spent that construction in both Chapter 125 and Chapter 130, five chapters apart, and it is now spent once, in Chapter 130, where §8 puts it. In Chapter 125 its place is taken by the number travelling: the roll has the sixty once, his book has the same sixty a second time, one of the two places can be contradicted and the other cannot, and a number that travels is a person who has stopped being a person.** A later writer may not change sixty to a hearth figure or set it beside an Ash one, may not re-state the wound-and-promise antithesis in either chapter's words, and may not build a beat on it.
312. **THE NOT ASKING AS SUBJECT.** Chapter 126: the slate woman asks when the sixty will be asked; Munn answers that widening the oath risks it and keeping it ends them; the minute enters the question raised and not decided. Aled Rhys states his want, pay on the day the water goes down, in nine short true sentences, and does not vote. The yard's four men are the best crew in the county, not on the bench, not on the oath, never asked what they swore.
313. **THE VAULT, UNNAMED.** Chapter 127: the stone room behind the chapel holds nine sealed jars of wheat ungrown forty years and a wall with two earlier answers built up, kept by a woman of sixty-six who refuses the cooperative's thanks and wants the seed in jars and not in a wall. She is one person and there is nobody else in the vault. Her name is not spent here; it is Batch 0014's per §14.
314. **THE LEAGUE, AND THE SEVENTY-TWO PENCE.** Chapter 128: Ordmar Gace, 49, twelve short acres, wants a man to say what he will not plant and keep his field; he is right about the wages and with the council on money and against it on the lease. Nine days at eight pence foots 24 + 24 + 24 = 72 pence, eight bushels of salt. A later writer may not rename Gace or give him office or oath.
315. **THE FIRST PROPOSAL THAT IS NOT HIS.** Chapter 129: Gace proposes an oath that can be broken, on purpose, in public, by its swearers, on a day named in advance, with a release, a consequence, and no office behind it, the consequence being a list of households to be helped. It is entered without opinion. The volume turns here and a later writer may not give the proposal to Aren or to an office.
316. **ASH'S FOURTH TERM, OFFERED WRONGLY AND CORRECTED.** Chapter 130: Aren offers that a person struck may answer and answering restores, knowing within a breath and a half it is the wrong instrument, wound against promise; the council declines without reproach and the minute enters offer and declining as facts. He refuses the calendar conversion in his notebook, which is the volume's one conversion attempt and its refusal as plant. **He then enters one line under the refusal, which is the first decision he has made in this county: that he will carry nothing else up those stairs unless a person on it asks him by name, and it is a withdrawal from the room and not an absence from the county.** Nine days later a two-line fair copy comes down the line unasked for and with nothing sent after it to say it was coming, in a hand not a clerk's, in the hand of the woman four hundred miles off who keeps the road at that end, and the leaf says at its own foot that it is a true copy of four lines out of a minute of a bench in the clay county at that end of the road and gives the room and the hour so that anybody who doubts it can go and stand in the room and look; it gives the keeping and the line we did not lose our register we lost our refusal and a register is what you have instead. **Amended at the Batch 0013 review, and this is the amendment the review asked for: the sender and the author are two people and the chapter now says so on the page. The sender is the woman who keeps the road at that end, who is a copyist and is not a clerk, which is what §8 Block 3's card has always said and which no continuity item previously supported. The author of the four lines is the woman of about sixty on three hearths in the clay county, whose fourth column says *wife* and is not a trade, and whose entry is the second in nineteen years in that office against a person by name and is not a removal (item 224, and item 280 for the four lines and the county four hundred miles the other way). She is a different person from the Marches' own woman of sixty on three hearths of Chapters 119, 122, 125, 126 and 129, and the echo of the description between them is deliberate and is not a continuity error; a later writer may not merge the two women, may not call the road-keeper the author, and may not turn the hand of the Ninebridge into the woman of the clay county.** He knows now where he read it, and who wrote it, and the county does not know he knows. The ordinal is fourth and not fifth; the fifth is a different instrument and is Block 4's. **The phrase `scheduled day` is not used in Chapter 130 and may not be used of anything Chapter 118 has closed, which is that no day has been named for a line.**
317. **FIGURES AND DEVICE COUNTS FOR THE BATCH.** Zero `---` rules and zero fully-bolded standalone lines in the block, against ceilings of six and twenty; the uniqueness sub-clause is recorded as arithmetically impossible and set aside in the Batch 0013 entry. No System panel and no Gatehouse line. Money foots as in items 307 and 314. No banned sums, no Ash figures, no Hushwater pairing, no main-world month or weekday anywhere in the ten chapters. **Amended at the Batch 0013 review: the block is 16,315 words after the repair pass, per chapter 1614 / 1765 / 1440 / 1129 / 2118 / 2632 / 2026 / 969 / 980 / 1642, and it is still 56 per cent of Batch 0012, which is a real deviation and not a benign one. The repair pass added no figure of any kind and no money; every number in the ten chapters is one that was on the page before it, and the two money columns foot as at items 307 and 314. The repair pass added no section rule and no bold standalone line, so the device counts stand. Filler counts across the block, for the next writer's baseline: `nodded` 9 in eight chapters, `held` 22, `wrote` 21, `stood` 32, `plain` 4, `counted` 3, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0, `the whole of` 0, `about N seconds` 0.**

318. **THE TWO DOUBTFUL, A MAN OF SIXTY-EIGHT, AND FOUR TRUE WORDS LEFT UNWRITTEN.** Chapter 125: one of the two doubtful marks on the roll is a thumb in a flood year, written in a barn with the water under the door, and the man of sixty-eight who has been on the ground fifty-one years refuses to have it corrected, for a reason he gives exactly once and asks nothing for: **he has wanted nothing off this county since he was nine, that is his whole holding and it is not in the book, and a man who says out loud that he wants nothing has said it in order to be given something.** He would be asked and would stay silent, and would want the asking to be done by somebody who has to write down that he stayed silent, because **a book that says a man said nothing is a book that says a man agreed, and asking would have made them agree by asking, with the best intentions in the world.** The four true words are *I am on the roll, I hold no water, nobody has ever asked me, that is all of it.* **Aren decides in the yard that they go in his own hand or in nobody's, and does not enter them anywhere, and tells nobody in the county that he has decided it. A later writer may not enter those four words in any book, may not have them said a second time to a second person, and may not have Aren change his mind inside this volume.** The minute-man nevertheless enters the answer, because Aren tells him on the chamber steps that one of the two said nothing at all and that this is the answer, and it is the first entry that man has made in twenty-two years that is a silence. **The not-askings counter in volume terms is separate and unchanged: nine stands, no tenth is entered, and a household's silence on a roll is not a not-asking.**

319. **THE GATEWAY, THE BOY OF ELEVEN, AND FOUR ANSWERS THAT ARE IN NO BOOK.** Chapter 126: the minute-man states the dilemma first, in his own voice, before anything is entered — he can keep a fact about a person and a fact about the council but cannot keep a question, and **a household asked and answering nothing will be entered as a household that did not care to be asked, in a good hand, by a man who writes down what happens.** Then a boy of eleven comes up the stair from one of the four white villages with one question, asked in the gateway because the household would not say it in the room: **whether being asked would put them on a list.** Four people answer it in about a minute — Munn, that there is no list; the slate woman, that there is no column for it and never has been; Ferrow, that the roll is a roll of households that pay and nothing else; the drawknife man, that his yard asked a man's father to sign a paper once, got nothing and stopped asking, and a yard that kept a list would be a different kind of yard. **All four are telling the truth and not one of them writes a line.** Aren gives the boy the answer it was sent for, which is that in the county he came from a man is a number in a column and a person can be moved out of a count by a man with a pen, lawfully, cheaply and quickly, and the boy is frightened by the end of it, at the three words, because a child from a county where nothing has ever been refused has no word for the difference between a thing that is fast and a thing that is only fast. Ferrow, on the stair: **four voices in a gateway and no book in the county will ever hold the answer, and in five rollings when a list does come along half of four villages will remember we said there was none and will sign whatever is read out to them, and not because they were deceived, but because we were right.** The minute-man enters the question and refuses the four answers, because he did not hear them from the people who gave them, and says a man who writes down what he has not heard has stopped being a keeper and started being a clerk. **This is a plant for Block 5 and it is the load-bearing one: a list of households to be helped arrives in Chapters 141–143, and the difference between that and a list of persons to be struck is said in one sentence by the woman of sixty-six who keeps the seed vault. A later writer may not contradict the four answers, may not improve them, may not have anybody entered them in a book in this volume, and may not have the county learn that it was wrong about the list before Block 5.** Aren's own notebook line about the gateway is the one that says four answers are in no book and that a sentence said in a gateway in this county cannot be taken back and cannot be corrected.

320. **THREE MASON'S MARKS ON THE OUTSIDE OF THE SEED VAULT'S WALL.** Chapter 127, cut in the afternoon with a cold chisel off Ferrow's rack and three bits of tin, on the outside only and above the line the water came to last year, with the keeper's leave and against his better judgement: **the height of the older floor, the flood line itself, and low down the floor inside the third course.** The low one is the reason — a man in a flooded room with a lantern is not looking for a floor to stand on, he is looking for the floor above his own head, and there is one three feet off the ground, and if nobody has told him he will put his lantern through it. **The keeper keeps the measure in her own head, because a measure in a book becomes an office and a measure in a head is a woman of sixty-six, and the county's whole idea of a record has not been contradicted, only joined.** She says a scar is a thing a wall can have and an answer is a thing a wall cannot have; that she has been keeping the wall forty years and was never once asked to keep it from anybody; and that she has not decided whether she has been made a better keeper or a worse one and will decide in about a year with the water down. On the way out she says there is now a mark on the room and no keeper of it but the two of them, **that the water cannot read marks, and that the wall is the only thing in this county that does not need a book to be read, and that she has been wrong about that for forty years.** **The marks are the batch's only physical act of record, they are unpaid, unmeasured in any book, and unread; a later writer may not explain them, may not copy them into any record, may not have the keeper change her mind inside this volume, and must not let the water read them — but a man with a lantern in the dark near the seed room in Block 4 is entitled to find them, and the near side of the vault being saved in Chapters 134–136 is the volume's pay-off for them and not a coincidence.**

321. **THE WITHDRAWAL, ENTERED WITH THE HOUR, AND A STAGING AT SEVEN.** Chapter 130: after the refused conversion, Aren enters one line in his own book, **the first decision he has made in this county, that he will carry nothing else up those stairs unless a person on it asks him by name.** The drawknife man then asks him how long he is here and is answered honestly and badly: he does not know what he is doing in the spring, nobody in that chamber has asked him for one thing, and he carried one thing up those stairs and it was wrong. **The man takes the sill as evidence and not as a promise, because a yard that took promises kept them in a book and the county it works in has not got one, and Aren is on the staging at seven.** The withdrawal is a withdrawal from the room and not an exit from the county: **a later writer may not have him carry a second instrument up those stairs in this volume without a person asking him by name, and may not make the withdrawal permanent, and Block 5's command in Chapters 137–138 is a second instrument and the council's refusal of it is what makes it legible.**

322. **FLAGGED AND NOT ANSWERED: HOW TOVAN KEST IS IN THE ROOM IN CHAPTER 145.** Raised at the Batch 0013 review, from a contradiction between `outline/volume-03.md` §13's Ch 131–140 row and §8 Block 4 Ch 137–138, and recorded in the outline with a reconciliation note. **§13's row is corrected to Coll Ferrow and the guild, which is Block 4's hand-over. §8 Ch 145, §9 and `outline/series.md`'s Volume 03 climax all keep Tovan Kest for Block 5, and §14 puts him in the Block 5 tail, and Tovan is four hundred miles off in Merefen and only four men of his yard and no boy came into the Marches.** The scene the outline fixes — a road given away in a room, in the county's own form, with a date and an hour, and no column for a road warden's vote, and neither man using the word brother — survives all three routes: he travels, which costs rollings the volume has not got; the hand-over is made to a yard he holds in Merefen, in a room on this side of the line, which is a colder and better scene; or the crew's own authority carries the guild's and the guild's carries the road, with the brother named in a document and not in the room. **Batch 0015 decides it on the page. No writer may resolve this in a state file, in a summary, or in an assertion, and it is not a new enemy and not a new plot.**

## Relationship state at the end of Chapter 130

- **Aren and Mara:** unchanged and unrepaired. No meeting, no letter, no message in the block; the ninth not-asking stands and no tenth is entered; her withdrawn marginal note, the three rules, the reply in a bag, and the four concealments all stand as at Chapter 120.
- **Aren and Tovan:** unchanged and unhealed. The yard's four men are in the county on its own calendar, the best crew in it, holding staging work; the drawknife man holds a line with Aren and asks nothing about anchors; no brother language is used and the spent sentence is not re-said or alluded to.
- **Aren and the hand of the Ninebridge:** unchanged. No call, no message; the two-line fair copy is in her hand though not in her name on the page, and he now knows the source of the sentence he has carried since Embermonth and the name of the woman who wrote it, which is a different person from her; the county does not know he knows.
- **Aren and Dain:** untouched. No appearance, no explanation of the clause, no change of want; her refusal stands for Chapters 148–150.
- **Aren and the Marches council:** refused a second time, accepted on its own terms; his rider figure and his Ash term both declined without reproach; Gace's proposal entered without opinion; the sixty and the vault now stand in the book as facts. **New at the end of the block: he has entered in his own book that he will carry nothing else up those stairs unless a person asks him by name, and the council does not know he has withdrawn.
